
### 206a7fe7f21343d89ca83e03a0e4dc7e670cd381:tools/test_v0_s3_native_custody_correction10.py
00001 """The frozen regressions of the N1 correction 10 of the native custody.
00002 
00003 This module carries the **14 frozen oracle groups** of the root package
00004 ``reports/v0-s3/product-gate0/native-custody-correction-10`` (the file
00005 ``regression-obligations.json``).  The obligations are frozen by the
00006 root and they are not written by the implementer: every group below is
00007 mapped to the production owner that has to carry it and to the test
00008 that drives it.
00009 
00010 The rules of this module are the rules of the whole N1 package.
00011 
00012 * It is **offline**.  It never starts a process, it never opens a
00013   socket, it never touches a database and it never reads the original
00014   budget ledger or the original shared counter.  The Windows primitives
00015   are driven through an **in-process** table of primitives that is
00016   injected into the production façade: the production code that is
00017   checked here is the code of a real run, only the native library is
00018   missing.  The only seam that is injected is the **lowest** one: the
00019   primitive table of the façade of the Windows API, the file objects
00020   of the ends of the channel and the frame of the peer.  The public
00021   production owners - the core, the loop, the channel, the endpoints,
00022   the workers, the session, the launcher and the custody of the
00023   launcher - are the real ones.
00024 
00025 * Every case drives the **actual production method**: the production
00026   ``WindowsApi`` with an injected primitive table, the production
00027   ``BrokerCore``, ``BrokerLoop``, ``BrokerProcess``, ``StreamChannel``,
00028   ``_WindowsPipeWorker``, ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint``, ``_StartupOwner``,
00029   ``_LauncherCustody``, ``_LaunchAggregate`` and the production
00030   ``NativeCustodySession``.  A façade that only returns the right story
00031   would not prove anything and it is not used for the failure paths.
00032 
00033 * Every case records the state of the resources **at the moment the
00034   product returned**, before any salvage of the test owner.  The
00035   cleanup of the test owner is explicit and it is never used to
00036   declare that the product returned cleanly: a product that could not
00037   release a resource stays unknown and keeps its original reference.
00038   Every thread that the test itself started is joined by the test with
00039   a **bounded** wait, in every case and unconditionally.
00040 
00041 * The native claims of this package stay ``NOT_PROVEN``.  The module
00042   proves the behaviour of the Python owners and it never claims that a
00043   Windows API, a job object, a GPU, a port or a service was executed.
00044 
00045 The six Majors of the read only pre review stay **OPEN**: this module
00046 is a regression set and it never signs a Major.  The closure of a
00047 Major belongs to a new independent review and to the gate owner.
00048 
00049 The boundary of the process is not installed here: the guard of the
00050 batch lives in the report directory of the batch and the runner of the
00051 batch is its only authorised entry.
00052 """

00165 def write_phase(phase, document):
00166     """Write the bounded facts of one phase of this module."""
00167     EVIDENCE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
00168     path = EVIDENCE_DIR / ('n10-%s.json' % phase)

00170                                 default=repr), encoding='utf-8')

00179 def _chain(api, document, plan, tmp_path, *, clock=None):
00180     """Build the **real** chain of one round.
00181 
00182     The chain is the one of the production launcher: the frames of the
00183     round are published first, then the endpoints, then the channel and
00184     then the handle of the broker.  Nothing of the chain is replaced by
00185     a stand-in and the facts of the close are the facts of the
00186     production.
00187 
00188     The clock of the chain may be a clock that only moves when the
00189     owner of the test moves it: the deadline of the round is then a
00190     fact of the test and not a fact of the wall of the host.
00191     """
00192     return _f5_build_chain(api, document, plan, clock or time.monotonic,
00193                            time.time, tmp_path)

00196 def write_phase_not_run(phase, reason):
00197     """Mark one phase that was **not** executed.
00198 
00199     A phase that did not run is never written as if it had run: the
00200     document names the reason of the failure and it carries **no** fact
00201     of the phase.  A document of a phase that never ran is never a
00202     fabricated expectation of the phase.
00203     """
00204     return write_phase(phase, {'status': 'NOT_RUN', 'reason': reason})

00246 class _OwnerRegistry:
00247     """The registry of the owners that the fixture of this module starts.
00248 
00249     The registry is **immediate**: an owner is registered at the moment
00250     of its construction or of its start and it is never discovered
00251     later from a product that may have dropped it.  A channel whose
00252     construction failed half way, a drain thread whose start took
00253     effect and a worker that the product retained are all in the
00254     registry, and the salvage of the fixture joins every one of them
00255     with an explicitly bounded wait.
00256 
00257     The registry owns **nothing**: it only remembers.  The product
00258     keeps its own owners and its own returned state, and the registry
00259     never claims that the product released a resource.
00260 
00261     CORRECTION 17 (M3): the registry also holds the **provenance** of
00262     every start that the observer of this module saw.  The provenance
00263     is the proof of the start of an owner: an owner whose start was
00264     never invoked is ``NEVER_STARTED``, an owner whose start did not
00265     return is ``UNKNOWN`` and an owner whose start returned is
00266     ``STARTED``.  The provenance is keyed by the **exact** thread
00267     object and it is never inferred from a name.
00268     """
00269 
00270     def __init__(self):
00271         self._lock = threading.Lock()
00272         self._entries = []
00273         self._sequence = 0
00274         self._threads = {}
00275 
00276     def register(self, name, owner, *, kind, seam):
00277         """Register one owner **immediately** and return its entry."""
00278         with self._lock:
00279             self._sequence += 1
00280             entry = {'name': name, 'owner': owner, 'kind': kind,
00281                      'seam': seam, 'order': self._sequence,
00282                      'registered_at': time.monotonic(),
00283                      'start_state': None, 'start_error': None}
00284             self._entries.append(entry)
00285             return entry
00286 
00287     def mark_starting(self, thread):
00288         """Register the **exact** thread object before its start.
00289 
00290         The registration happens **before** the original start is
00291         called: a thread whose start does not return is an owner of the
00292         round from that moment on and it is never forgotten.
00293         """
00294         with self._lock:
00295             self._sequence += 1
00296             entry = {'name': 'thread:%s#%d' % (thread.name, id(thread)),
00297                      'owner': thread, 'kind': 'thread',
00298                      'seam': 'thread.start', 'order': self._sequence,
00299                      'registered_at': time.monotonic(),
00300                      'start_state': 'STARTING', 'start_error': None}
00301             self._entries.append(entry)
00302             self._threads[id(thread)] = entry
00303             return entry
00304 
00305     def mark_started(self, thread):
00306         """The start of the thread returned: the owner really started."""
00307         with self._lock:
00308             entry = self._threads.get(id(thread))
00309             if entry is not None:
00310                 entry['start_state'] = 'STARTED'
00311             return entry
00312 
00313     def mark_failed(self, thread, error):
00314         """The start of the thread did not return: the owner is unknown."""
00315         with self._lock:
00316             entry = self._threads.get(id(thread))
00317             if entry is not None:
00318                 entry['start_state'] = 'FAILED'
00319                 entry['start_error'] = {'type': type(error).__name__,
00320                                         'detail': str(error)}
00321             return entry
00322 
00323     def pending_starts(self):
00324         """The starts that did not return yet."""
00325         with self._lock:
00326             return sum(1 for item in self._entries
00327                        if item['kind'] == 'thread'
00328                        and item['start_state'] == 'STARTING')
00329 
00330     def start_provenance(self):
00331         """The proven provenance of every start that the observer saw."""
00332         with self._lock:
00333             return {id(item['owner']): item['start_state']
00334                     for item in self._entries if item['kind'] == 'thread'}
00335 
00336     def generation(self):
00337         """The number of the owners that the registry holds.
00338 
00339         The generation is the **witness** of the quiescence of the
00340         round: a generation that did not change across a final
00341         resnapshot means that no new owner appeared after it.
00342         """
00343         with self._lock:
00344             return len(self._entries)
00345 
00346     def entries(self):
00347         """The entries of the registry in the order of their registration."""
00348         with self._lock:
00349             return list(self._entries)
00350 
00351     def observed_threads(self):
00352         """The **exact** thread objects that this registry observed.
00353 
00354         CORRECTION 20 (M3): this view is **independent** of the query
00355         of ``entries``: a failure of that query never loses these
00356         references and the fallback of the drain of a round never
00357         starts from an empty list.
00358         """
00359         with self._lock:
00360             return [(item['name'], item['owner'])
00361                     for item in self._threads.values()]
00362 
00363     def facts(self):
00364         """The facts of the registry: an owner is named and never guessed."""
00365         return [{'name': item['name'], 'kind': item['kind'],
00366                  'seam': item['seam'], 'order': item['order'],
00367                  'start_state': item['start_state']}
00368                 for item in self.entries()]
00369

00371 class _StartObserver:
00372     """The observer of the starts of the threads of one round.
00373 
00374     The observer is installed around the **original**
00375     ``threading.Thread.start``: it forwards every start and it never
00376     refuses one, it never blocks one and it never changes a decision of
00377     the production.  It only **observes** the start and it registers the
00378     **exact** thread object at the moment of the start, so that a
00379     thread that the product starts during the stop of the round is an
00380     owner of the round and it is never forgotten.
00381 
00382     The scope of the observer is the **actual** round that the fixture
00383     builds: the observer is armed before the round is started and it is
00384     disarmed only after the salvage of that round has finished.
00385     """
00386 
00387     def __init__(self, registry):
00388         self._registry = registry
00389         self._original = threading.Thread.start
00390         self.armed = False
00391         self.observed = []
00392 
00393     def install(self):
00394         """The context manager that installs the observer."""
00395         observer = self
00396 
00397         def observed_start(thread, *args, **kwargs):
00398             if not observer.armed:
00399                 return observer._original(thread, *args, **kwargs)
00400             observer._registry.mark_starting(thread)
00401             try:
00402                 result = observer._original(thread, *args, **kwargs)
00403             except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00404                 observer._registry.mark_failed(thread, error)
00405                 raise
00406             observer._registry.mark_started(thread)
00407             observer.observed.append({'id': id(thread), 'name': thread.name})
00408             return result
00409 
00410         return unittest.mock.patch.object(threading.Thread, 'start',
00411                                           observed_start)
00412 
00413     def arm(self):
00414         """The observation of the starts begins."""
00415         self.armed = True
00416 
00417     def disarm(self):
00418         """The observation of the starts ends."""
00419         self.armed = False
00420

00422 class _PhaseLedger:
00423     """The ledger of the phases that the group of this module runs.
00424 
00425     The ledger remembers what was **entered** and what **ended**.  It
00426     never decides: a phase that was entered and that failed is a
00427     ``FAIL`` and only a phase that was never entered is a ``NOT_RUN``.
00428     The states are ``PLANNED``, ``ATTEMPTED``, ``PASSED``, ``FAIL`` and
00429     ``NOT_RUN`` and the two last states are terminal.
00430     """
00431 
00432     STATES = ('PLANNED', 'ATTEMPTED', 'PASSED', 'FAIL', 'NOT_RUN')
00433 
00434     def __init__(self):
00435         self._lock = threading.Lock()
00436         self._states = {}
00437         self._order = []
00438         self._reasons = {}
00439 
00440     def _plan_locked(self, name):
00441         if name not in self._states:
00442             self._states[name] = 'PLANNED'
00443             self._order.append(name)
00444 
00445     def plan(self, name):
00446         """Name one phase **before** it runs."""
00447         with self._lock:
00448             self._plan_locked(name)
00449             return name
00450 
00451     def attempt(self, name):
00452         """Mark that the operation of the phase was **entered**."""
00453         with self._lock:
00454             self._plan_locked(name)
00455             if self._states[name] == 'PLANNED':
00456                 self._states[name] = 'ATTEMPTED'
00457             return self._states[name]
00458 
00459     def finish(self, name, state, reason=None):
00460         """Close one phase; a closed phase is never reopened."""
00461         with self._lock:
00462             self._plan_locked(name)
00463             if self._states[name] in ('PASSED', 'FAIL', 'NOT_RUN'):
00464                 return self._states[name]
00465             self._states[name] = state
00466             if reason is not None:
00467                 self._reasons[name] = reason
00468             return state
00469 
00470     def pass_(self, name):
00471         """The phase held every one of its checks."""
00472         return self.finish(name, 'PASSED')
00473 
00474     def fail(self, name, reason=None):
00475         """The phase was executed and it failed."""
00476         return self.finish(name, 'FAIL', reason)
00477 
00478     def state(self, name):
00479         """The state of one phase."""
00480         with self._lock:
00481             return self._states.get(name, 'PLANNED')
00482 
00483     def names(self):
00484         """The names of the phases in the order of their planning."""
00485         with self._lock:
00486             return list(self._order)
00487 
00488     def reason(self, name):
00489         """The reason of the failure of one phase."""
00490         with self._lock:
00491             return self._reasons.get(name)
00492 
00493 
00494 class _RegistryCustody:
00495     """The custody seam of the fixture: it registers what it retains.
00496 
00497     The custody of the launcher is a **supported** seam of the
00498     production: a channel registers itself with it **before** it builds
00499     anything.  The seam of the fixture forwards every call to the
00500     original custody of the round and it registers the retained owner
00501     in the registry of the fixture at the moment of the retention.
00502 
00503     A channel whose construction failed after its drain thread was
00504     started is therefore in the registry of the fixture even when the
00505     constructor never returned the channel to the fixture.
00506     """
00507 
00508     def __init__(self, registry, name, original=None):
00509         self._registry = registry
00510         self._name = name
00511         self._original = original
00512 
00513     def retain(self, kind, resource, detail=None, launch=None):
00514         self._registry.register('%s:%s' % (self._name, kind), resource,
00515                                 kind=kind, seam='custody.retain')
00516         if self._original is None:
00517             return resource
00518         # The **original** signature of the custody is honoured: the
00519         # aggregate of a launch takes three arguments and the table of
00520         # the launcher takes four.
00521         return self._original.retain(kind, resource, detail)
00522 
00523     def forget(self, resource):
00524         if self._original is None:
00525             return None
00526         return self._original.forget(resource)
00527 
00528 
00529 def _owner_start_state(thread, hint=None):
00530     """The start state of one owner of the round.
00531 
00532     The state is one of ``'STARTED'``, ``'NEVER_STARTED'`` or
00533     ``'UNKNOWN'``.  ``UNKNOWN`` is the state of an owner whose start
00534     did not return and of an owner whose liveness could not be asked:
00535     such an owner may still come up, it is never taken for an owner
00536     that never started and it **fails the proof** of the cleanup.
00537 
00538     CORRECTION 17 (M3): ``NEVER_STARTED`` is returned **only** when the
00539     provenance of the start **proves** it: the owner does not exist at
00540     all, or the registry of the fixture observed that its start was
00541     never invoked.  An owner without an identity and without a
00542     provenance is ``UNKNOWN``: it is never guessed to be an owner that
00543     never started.
00544     """
00545     if thread is None:
00546         return 'NEVER_STARTED'
00547     # CORRECTION 18 (M3): the query of the identity of the owner is
00548     # **not** swallowed here any more.  A failure of it is a fact of the
00549     # cleanup and the guard of the caller records it with its exact type
00550     # and its exact detail: a silent ``UNKNOWN`` without a diagnostic
00551     # would hide the failure of the classification and it would let an
00552     # unknown identity pass for a proved exit.
00553     ident = getattr(thread, 'ident', None)
00554     if ident is not None:
00555         return 'STARTED'
00556     if hint in ('STARTED', 'NEVER_STARTED', 'UNKNOWN'):
00557         return hint
00558     return 'UNKNOWN'
00559 
00560 
00561 def _salvage_threads(owners, *, seconds=10.0, states=None, total_seconds=30.0,
00562                      interrupts=None):
00563     """Join the owners that really started and report their liveness.
00564 
00565     The salvage is the work of the **test** and it is **separate** from
00566     the returned state of the product: it never renews a deadline of
00567     the round, it never erases a retained resource of the product and
00568     it never claims that the product released it.
00569 
00570     Every owner that really started is joined with an explicitly
00571     bounded wait and its liveness is reported.  An owner that never
00572     started is reported and **never** joined.  An owner whose start
00573     state is ``UNKNOWN`` is reported as unknown: it is never joined as
00574     if it had ended and it fails the proof of the cleanup.
00575 
00576     CORRECTION 17 (M3): **every** owner is attempted.  A failure of the
00577     classification of an owner, of its join or of its liveness query
00578     never aborts the iteration: the error is **retained** in a list
00579     with its kind, its exact type and its detail, and it fails the
00580     proof of the cleanup.  The wait of an owner is cropped by the
00581     remaining budget of the salvage, so that the salvage is bounded as
00582     a whole.
00583 
00584     CORRECTION 18 (M3): the **exact** object of a ``KeyboardInterrupt``
00585     or of a ``SystemExit`` is retained in the ``interrupts`` list of the
00586     caller: the identity of the cancellation survives every best effort
00587     cleanup and it is re-raised by the owner of the cleanup when no
00588     other failure is unwinding.  The error that is written in the
00589     document keeps its exact type and its exact detail; the **object**
00590     itself never enters a document.
00591     """
00592     facts = []
00593     hints = states or {}
00594     deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, total_seconds)
00595     for name, thread in owners:
00596         state = None
00597         errors = []
00598         try:
00599             state = _owner_start_state(thread, hints.get(name))
00600         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00601             errors.append({'kind': 'STATE', 'type': type(error).__name__,
00602                            'detail': str(error)})
00603             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00604             state = 'UNKNOWN'
00605         if state != 'STARTED':
00606             facts.append({'name': name, 'start_state': state,
00607                           'started': None if state == 'UNKNOWN' else False,
00608                           'alive': None, 'joined': None, 'errors': errors})
00609             continue
00610         try:
00611             wait = max(0.0, min(seconds, deadline - time.monotonic()))
00612             thread.join(wait)
00613         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00614             errors.append({'kind': 'JOIN', 'type': type(error).__name__,
00615                            'detail': str(error)})
00616             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00617         alive = None
00618         try:
00619             alive = bool(thread.is_alive())
00620         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00621             errors.append({'kind': 'LIVENESS', 'type': type(error).__name__,
00622                            'detail': str(error)})
00623             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00624         facts.append({'name': name, 'start_state': state, 'started': True,
00625                       'alive': alive, 'joined': (alive is False),
00626                       'errors': errors})
00627     return facts
00628 
00629 
00630 def _note_interruption(interrupts, error):
00631     """Retain the **exact** object of an interruption of the cleanup.
00632 
00633     A ``KeyboardInterrupt`` and a ``SystemExit`` are the identity of the
00634     cancellation of the round: they are retained as the objects
00635     themselves and they are re-raised by the owner of the cleanup when
00636     no other failure is unwinding.
00637     """
00638     if interrupts is None:
00639         return False
00640     if isinstance(error, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit)):
00641         interrupts.append(error)
00642         return True
00643     return False
00644 
00645 
00646 def _interruption_facts(interrupts):
00647     """The **durable** diagnostics of the exact interruptions.
00648 
00649     CORRECTION 19 (M3): the exact object of a cancellation never enters a
00650     document - only its exact type and its exact detail do.  The
00651     diagnostics of the cancellations of a cleanup are **kept** in the
00652     document of the cleanup: they are never reduced to a type and a
00653     detail of the last error and they are never discarded next to an
00654     original failure.
00655     """
00656     return [{'type': type(item).__name__, 'detail': repr(item)}
00657             for item in (interrupts or [])]
00658 
00659 
00660 def _release_gate(action, errors=None, interrupts=None, kind='GATE'):
00661     """Release **one** gate, guarded.
00662 
00663     CORRECTION 20 (M3): the release of a gate is **one action per
00664     gate**.  A cancellation of one gate never skips the gate that
00665     follows it, it never skips the closes, the stop, the joins, the
00666     proof or the evidence that follow it, and it never replaces the
00667     failure that is already unwinding.  The **exact** object of the
00668     cancellation is retained and the owner of the cleanup re-raises the
00669     **first** one of them when no other failure is unwinding.
00670     """
00671     try:
00672         action()
00673     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00674         if errors is not None:
00675             errors.append({'kind': kind, 'type': type(error).__name__,
00676                            'detail': str(error)})
00677         _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00678         return error
00679     return None
00680 
00681 
00682 def _raise_first_interruption(interrupts):
00683     """Re-raise the **first** exact pending interruption of a cleanup.
00684 
00685     CORRECTION 20 (M3): the priority of the failures of a cleanup is
00686     fixed: the failure that is already unwinding wins, then the
00687     **first** exact pending interruption of the cleanup and only then an
00688     ordinary assertion.  A cancellation of the cleanup is never reduced
00689     to a type and a detail and it is never discarded.
00690     """
00691     if interrupts and sys.exc_info()[0] is None:
00692         raise interrupts[0]
00693 
00694 
00695 def _observed_thread_refs(registry, errors=None, interrupts=None):
00696     """The **exact** thread objects that the registry observed.
00697 
00698     CORRECTION 20 (M3): the fallback of the owners of a round does
00699     **not** depend on the query of the entries of the registry.  The
00700     exact thread objects that the registry observed are read from an
00701     **independent** view and a failure of the query of the entries
00702     never replaces them with an empty list: the drain always starts
00703     with the references that are really known.
00704     """
00705     if registry is None:
00706         return []
00707     refs = []
00708     try:
00709         getter = getattr(registry, 'observed_threads', None)
00710         if getter is not None:
00711             refs = [(name, thread) for name, thread in getter()]
00712     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00713         if errors is not None:
00714             errors.append({'kind': 'REGISTRY_OBSERVED',
00715                            'type': type(error).__name__,
00716                            'detail': str(error)})
00717         _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00718         refs = []
00719     if not refs:
00720         # the **last** independent view of the registry: the exact
00721         # objects that it holds are read without its own query.
00722         try:
00723             held = getattr(registry, '_threads', None)
00724             if isinstance(held, dict):
00725                 refs = [(item.get('name'), item.get('owner'))
00726                         for item in held.values()
00727                         if isinstance(item, dict)]
00728         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
00729             if errors is not None:
00730                 errors.append({'kind': 'REGISTRY_THREADS',
00731                                'type': type(error).__name__,
00732                                'detail': str(error)})
00733             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00734             refs = []
00735     return [(name, thread) for name, thread in refs if thread is not None]
00736 
00737 
00738 def _assert_salvage_proved(facts, message):
00739     """The proof that the fixture released every owner that it started.
00740 
00741     An owner with an **unknown** start state fails the proof: it is
00742     never taken for an owner that never started and it is never taken
00743     for an owner that ended.  An owner that met an error fails the
00744     proof as well: a cleanup that met an error is never a success.
00745     """
00746     for item in facts:
00747         assert item['start_state'] != 'UNKNOWN', \
00748             '%s: the owner %s has an unknown start state' \
00749             % (message, item['name'])
00750         errors = item.get('errors') or []
00751         assert not errors, \
00752             '%s: the owner %s met %s' % (message, item['name'], errors)
00753         if item['started']:
00754             assert item['alive'] is False, \
00755                 '%s: the owner %s was left behind' % (message, item['name'])
00756 
00757 
00758 def _prove_salvage(facts, message):
00759     """The proof of the cleanup of a round, as a **fact**.
00760 
00761     The proof is **run** in every case - also while an ordinary failure
00762     is already unwinding - and it returns the failure of the proof
00763     instead of raising it: a failure of the proof never replaces the
00764     failure that is already unwinding and it is never discarded.
00765     """
00766     for item in facts:
00767         errors = item.get('errors') or []
00768         if item['start_state'] == 'UNKNOWN':
00769             return '%s: the owner %s has an unknown start state%s' \
00770                 % (message, item['name'], _errors_suffix(errors))
00771         if errors:
00772             return '%s: the owner %s met %s' \
00773                 % (message, item['name'], errors)
00774         if item['started'] and item['alive'] is not False:
00775             return '%s: the owner %s was left behind' % (message, item['name'])
00776     return None
00777 
00778 
00779 def _errors_suffix(errors):
00780     """The diagnostic of the errors of one owner of the round."""
00781     if not errors:
00782         return ''
00783     return ' (%s)' % (errors,)
00784 
00785 
00786 def _capture_stop(session, original_broker=None, *, interrupts=None):
00787     """The **returned** state of the public stop of a round.
00788 
00789     The result of the stop is captured **before** the salvage of the
00790     fixture: the salvage is the work of the test and it never replaces
00791     the returned state of the product.  An exception of the stop is
00792     captured and it is never discarded.
00793 
00794     CORRECTION 17 (M3): the live state and the retention of the round
00795     are read **now**, in the moment of the failure, and the **actual**
00796     facts of the evidence of the session are kept with their **real**
00797     keys.  The identities of the retained resource are the live objects
00798     of the session: the retained broker is compared with the broker of
00799     the round that was captured **before** the stop, and a broker that
00800     is gone is ``None`` and never the identity of ``None``.
00801 
00802     CORRECTION 18 (M3): a stop that raised and a snapshot of the
00803     evidence that failed are **facts of the cleanup** and they never
00804     certify it: the helper ``_stop_proof_failure`` turns them into the
00805     failure of the proof.  The **exact** object of an interruption is
00806     retained in the ``interrupts`` list of the caller.
00807     """
00808     captured = {'returned': None, 'exception': None, 'live_state': None,
00809                 'evidence': None, 'evidence_state': None,
00810                 'evidence_broker_retained': None,
00811                 'evidence_failures': None,
00812                 'evidence_background_threads': None,
00813                 'evidence_closer_start_state': None,
00814                 'retained_broker_is_original': None,
00815                 'retained_broker_id': None, 'snapshot_error': None}
00816     if session is None:
00817         return captured
00818     try:
00819         captured['returned'] = session.stop()
00820     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
00821         captured['exception'] = {'type': type(error).__name__,
00822                                  'detail': str(error)}
00823         _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00824     try:
00825         captured['live_state'] = session.state
00826         broker = getattr(session, 'broker', None)
00827         captured['retained_broker_id'] = None if broker is None else id(broker)
00828         if original_broker is not None:
00829             captured['retained_broker_is_original'] = \
00830                 (broker is original_broker)
00831         evidence = session.evidence()
00832         captured['evidence'] = evidence
00833         captured['evidence_state'] = evidence['state']
00834         captured['evidence_broker_retained'] = evidence['broker_retained']
00835         captured['evidence_failures'] = list(evidence['failures'])
00836         captured['evidence_background_threads'] = list(
00837             evidence['background_threads'])
00838         captured['evidence_closer_start_state'] = evidence['closer_start_state']
00839     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
00840         captured['snapshot_error'] = {'type': type(error).__name__,
00841                                       'detail': str(error)}
00842         _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
00843     return captured
00844 
00845 
00846 def _stop_proof_failure(captured, message):
00847     """The failure of the cleanup that the capture of the stop carries.
00848 
00849     A stop that raised and a snapshot of the evidence that failed are
00850     **facts** of the cleanup: they are never ignored and they never
00851     certify that the round was cleaned.  The helper returns ``None``
00852     when the capture carries no error at all.
00853     """
00854     problems = []
00855     if captured.get('exception') is not None:
00856         problems.append('the stop of the round met %s'
00857                         % (captured['exception'],))
00858     if captured.get('snapshot_error') is not None:
00859         problems.append('the snapshot of the evidence of the round met %s'
00860                         % (captured['snapshot_error'],))
00861     if not problems:
00862         return None
00863     return '%s: %s' % (message, '; '.join(problems))
00864 
00865 
00866 def _join_details(*parts):
00867     """The detail of a failure: every part that carries a fact.
00868 
00869     CORRECTION 18 (M2): the original failure of the phase and every
00870     diagnostic of its cleanup are **joined**: neither of them replaces
00871     the other and none of them is dropped.
00872     """
00873     return '; '.join([part for part in parts if part])
00874 
00875 
00876 def _cleanup_detail(cleanup_errors, write_errors):
00877     """The diagnostic of the cleanup errors of one phase.
00878 
00879     CORRECTION 18 (M2): the errors of the cleanup are a **list** and
00880     they are never overwritten: the detail names **every** one of them
00881     and it names the errors of the writing of the evidence as well.
00882     """
00883     parts = []
00884     for item in cleanup_errors:
00885         parts.append('%s: %s' % (item.get('kind'), item.get('detail')))
00886     for item in write_errors:
00887         parts.append('the writing of the evidence of %s failed: %s'
00888                      % (item.get('name'), item.get('detail')))
00889     if not parts:
00890         return None
00891     return '; '.join(parts)
00892 
00893 
00894 def _session_owners(session, prefix='session', hints=None):
00895     """The owners that the session of the round really started.
00896 
00897     The registry is read from the session itself: the background
00898     threads of the round and the closer threads that it really built.
00899     A closer that never started is reported as such and it is never
00900     joined.  A thread whose start did not return is **UNKNOWN**: it
00901     keeps its owner in the session and it may still come up.
00902     """
00903     owners = []
00904     if session is None:
00905         return owners
00906     records = getattr(session, '_background_threads', None) or {}
00907     for kind in sorted(records):
00908         record = records[kind] or {}
00909         name = '%s:background-%s' % (prefix, str(kind))
00910         owners.append((name, record.get('thread')))
00911         if hints is None:
00912             continue
00913         state = record.get('state')
00914         if state == 'STARTING':
00915             hints[name] = 'UNKNOWN'
00916         elif state == 'FAILED' and not record.get('started'):
00917             hints[name] = 'NEVER_STARTED'
00918     closer_state = getattr(session, '_closer_start_state', None)
00919     for index, thread in enumerate(getattr(session, '_closer_threads', None)
00920                                    or []):
00921         name = '%s:closer-%d' % (prefix, index)
00922         owners.append((name, thread))
00923         if hints is not None and closer_state == 'STARTING':
00924             hints[name] = 'UNKNOWN'
00925     return owners
00926 
00927 
00928 def _endpoint_owners(prefix, endpoint, hints=None):
00929     """The owners that one endpoint of the round really started.
00930 
00931     The read worker and the write worker of the endpoint are the
00932     owners that the endpoint really started.  A worker that the
00933     endpoint **retained** is a worker whose start did not return: its
00934     state is **UNKNOWN** and it fails the proof of the cleanup.
00935     """
00936     owners = []
00937     if endpoint is None:
00938         return owners
00939     for role, label in (('_reader', 'read'), ('_writer', 'write')):
00940         worker = getattr(endpoint, role, None)
00941         if worker is None:
00942             continue
00943         owners.append(('%s:%s-worker' % (prefix, label), worker))
00944     for index, worker in enumerate(getattr(endpoint, '_retained', None)
00945                                    or []):
00946         name = '%s:retained-%d' % (prefix, index)
00947         owners.append((name, worker))
00948         if hints is not None:
00949             hints[name] = 'UNKNOWN'
00950     return owners
00951 
00952 
00953 def _channel_owners(channel, prefix, hints=None):
00954     """The owners that the channel of the round really started.
00955 
00956     The drain thread of the channel and the workers of its two
00957     endpoints are the owners of the round that really started.  A worker
00958     that was never started is reported as such and it is never joined.
00959     """
00960     owners = []
00961     if channel is None:
00962         return owners
00963     owners.append((prefix + ':drain', getattr(channel, '_thread', None)))
00964     if hints is not None:
00965         # The drain thread of a channel is started **inside** the
00966         # constructor of the channel: a channel whose construction
00967         # failed at the start of its drain owns a drain thread whose
00968         # start did not return and it may still come up.
00969         hints[prefix + ':drain'] = 'UNKNOWN'
00970     reader = getattr(channel, '_reader', None)
00971     writer = getattr(channel, '_writer', None)
00972     for endpoint, label in ((reader, 'read'), (writer, 'write')):
00973         owners.extend(_endpoint_owners('%s:%s' % (prefix, label), endpoint,
00974                                        hints))
00975     return owners
00976 
00977 
00978 def _registry_owners(registry, session=None, channel=None, prefix='round',
00979                       errors=None, interrupts=None):
00980     """Every owner of the round: the registered ones and the live ones.
00981 
00982     The registry of the fixture holds the owners that were registered
00983     **immediately** - a channel that was constructed, an endpoint that
00984     was built, a session that was created.  The session and the
00985     channel that are still reachable are added to them, so that a
00986     thread that the product started after the registration is never
00987     forgotten.  One owner is never in the list twice: the identity of
00988     the thread decides and not its name.
00989 
00990     CORRECTION 19 (M3): the traversal is **best effort**.  The query
00991     of one container never aborts the traversal and it never replaces
00992     the owners of the round with an empty list: every other owner is
00993     still attempted.  The **exact** thread objects that the start
00994     registry observed are retained **first** and they are an
00995     independent fallback - a failure of the query of a container never
00996     loses them.  Every failure is retained with its exact type and its
00997     exact detail and the **exact** object of an interruption is kept.
00998     """
00999     owners = []
01000     hints = {}
01001     seen = set()
01002     kinds = set()
01003 
01004     def add(items):
01005         for name, thread in items:
01006             key = ('thread', id(thread)) if thread is not None \
01007                 else ('name', name)
01008             if key in seen:
01009                 continue
01010             seen.add(key)
01011             owners.append((name, thread))
01012 
01013     def note(kind, error):
01014         """Retain one failure of the traversal, never swallow it."""
01015         if errors is not None:
01016             errors.append({'kind': kind, 'type': type(error).__name__,
01017                            'detail': str(error)})
01018         _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01019 
01020     # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the **exact** thread objects that the start
01021     # registry observed are read from an **independent** view **before**
01022     # the query of the entries: the fallback of the owners of the round
01023     # never depends on that query and it never starts from an empty
01024     # list.  The query of the entries may fail and the references that
01025     # are really known are still there.
01026     observed = _observed_thread_refs(registry, errors, interrupts)
01027     try:
01028         entries = registry.entries()
01029     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01030         note('REGISTRY_ENTRIES', error)
01031         entries = []
01032     # CORRECTION 19 (M3): the **exact** thread objects that the start
01033     # registry observed are retained **first** and they are an
01034     # independent fallback: a failure of the query of a container never
01035     # loses them and it never replaces the owners of the round with an
01036     # empty list.  They are merged into the owners **after** the
01037     # traversal, so that the name of an owner that a container really
01038     # knows is never replaced by the name of the registry.
01039     for entry in entries:
01040         name = entry.get('name')
01041         owner = entry.get('owner')
01042         kind = entry.get('kind')
01043         if kind == 'thread':
01044             add([(name, owner)])
01045             observed.append((name, owner))
01046             continue
01047         try:
01048             if kind == 'channel':
01049                 items = _channel_owners(owner, name, hints)
01050             elif kind == 'endpoint':
01051                 items = _endpoint_owners(name, owner, hints)
01052             elif kind == 'session':
01053                 items = _session_owners(owner, name, hints)
01054             else:
01055                 items = [(name, owner)]
01056         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01057             # the traversal continues: the owners that follow this one
01058             # are still attempted and the exact thread object of this
01059             # entry is still an owner of the round.
01060             note('OWNER_QUERY', error)
01061             if isinstance(owner, threading.Thread):
01062                 add([(name, owner)])
01063             continue
01064         kinds.add(kind)
01065         add(items)
01066     try:
01067         if session is not None and 'session' not in kinds:
01068             add(_session_owners(session, '%s:session' % prefix, hints))
01069     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01070         note('SESSION_QUERY', error)
01071     try:
01072         if channel is not None and 'channel' not in kinds:
01073             add(_channel_owners(channel, '%s:channel' % prefix, hints))
01074     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01075         note('CHANNEL_QUERY', error)
01076     # CORRECTION 19 (M3): the fallback of the observed threads: every
01077     # exact thread object that the start registry saw is an owner of the
01078     # round.  The merge is **idempotent**: an owner that the traversal
01079     # already named keeps its name and its hint.
01080     for name, thread in observed:
01081         add([(name, thread)])
01082     # CORRECTION 17 (M3): the provenance of the starts that the
01083     # observer of the fixture saw is the **proven** provenance of the
01084     # start of an owner.  It is keyed by the exact thread object and it
01085     # never replaces the hint of the production: a thread whose start
01086     # returned is ``STARTED`` and a thread whose start did not return is
01087     # ``UNKNOWN``.
01088     try:
01089         provenance = registry.start_provenance() if registry is not None else {}
01090     except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01091         note('PROVENANCE', error)
01092         provenance = {}
01093     for name, thread in owners:
01094         if thread is None:
01095             continue
01096         state = provenance.get(id(thread))
01097         if state == 'STARTED':
01098             hints[name] = 'STARTED'
01099         elif state in ('STARTING', 'FAILED'):
01100             hints[name] = 'UNKNOWN'
01101     return owners, hints
01102 
01103 
01104 def _drain_owners(registry, session=None, channel=None, prefix='round', *,
01105                   seconds=10.0, budget=30.0, release=None, interrupts=None):
01106     """The bounded drain of the owners of one round.
01107 
01108     CORRECTION 17 (M3): the drain repeats, **boundedly**, until the
01109     owners of the round are quiescent and really joined.  The bound of
01110     the drain is **one** overall bound: the wait of an owner is cropped
01111     by the remaining budget and the budget is never reset - neither
01112     between the rounds of the drain nor between the rounds of the test.
01113 
01114     The quiescence of the round is asserted **after** the drain and
01115     never before it: a round that is not quiescent is a failure of the
01116     cleanup, the gates of the round are still released and **all** the
01117     registered owners are still attempted.
01118 
01119     CORRECTION 18 (M3): the enumeration of the owners and the reading
01120     of the generation of the registry are **one** observation.  The
01121     generation is read **before** the exact owners are enumerated and
01122     it is read **again** right after the enumeration and once more
01123     after the joined producer threads of the iteration.  All of these
01124     readings must agree and the **final** exact-owner resnapshot must
01125     contain every owner of the enumeration: a thread that was started
01126     between the enumeration and the reading of the generation is never
01127     certified as quiescent and it is never left behind.
01128 
01129     Every error of the drain - the enumeration, the release of the
01130     gates, the classification of an owner, its join and its liveness
01131     query - is **retained** in a list with its exact type and its exact
01132     detail and it fails the proof of the cleanup.  **Every** iteration
01133     is kept: an error of an earlier iteration is never erased by a
01134     later iteration that really ended the threads.  A failure never
01135     aborts the iteration and it never skips the final result of the
01136     round.  The drain never renews a deadline of the product.
01137 
01138     CORRECTION 19 (M3): every read of the drain - the generation, the
01139     pending starts and the final resnapshot - is **best effort** and it
01140     never skips the owners that follow it.  The release of the gates is
01141     **one action per gate**: a failure of one gate never skips the gate
01142     that follows it.  The traversal of the owners never replaces the
01143     owners of the round with an empty list and the exact thread objects
01144     that the start registry observed are always retained.
01145     """
01146     deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget)
01147     attempts = []
01148     errors = []
01149     # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the drain starts with the references that are
01150     # **independently** known: a failure of the very first enumeration
01151     # never replaces the owners of the round with an empty list and the
01152     # partial child of the registry is still attempted.
01153     owners = _observed_thread_refs(registry, errors, interrupts)
01154     hints = {}
01155     salvage = []
01156     all_salvage = []
01157     quiescent = False
01158 
01159     def read_generation(label):
01160         """Read the generation of the registry, guarded.
01161 
01162         CORRECTION 19 (M3): the reading of the generation is **best
01163         effort**: a failure of it is a fact of the cleanup and it never
01164         skips the owners that follow it.
01165         """
01166         try:
01167             return registry.generation()
01168         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01169             errors.append({'kind': label, 'type': type(error).__name__,
01170                            'detail': str(error)})
01171             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01172             return None
01173 
01174     def read_pending(label):
01175         """Read the pending starts of the registry, guarded."""
01176         try:
01177             return registry.pending_starts()
01178         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01179             errors.append({'kind': label, 'type': type(error).__name__,
01180                            'detail': str(error)})
01181             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01182             return None
01183 
01184     while True:
01185         # the generation of the registry is read **before** the exact
01186         # owners are enumerated
01187         generation_before = read_generation('GENERATION_BEFORE')
01188         try:
01189             owners, hints = _registry_owners(registry, session=session,
01190                                               channel=channel, prefix=prefix,
01191                                               errors=errors,
01192                                               interrupts=interrupts)
01193         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01194             errors.append({'kind': 'ENUMERATION',
01195                            'type': type(error).__name__,
01196                            'detail': str(error)})
01197             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01198             # CORRECTION 19 (M3): the owners of the previous observation
01199             # are **retained**: a failure of the enumeration never loses
01200             # them and it never replaces them with an empty list.
01201         # ... and it is read **again** right after the enumeration: a
01202         # start that happened between the two readings is a change of
01203         # the generation and the enumeration is repeated.
01204         generation_after = read_generation('GENERATION_AFTER')
01205         snapshot = {'generation_before': generation_before,
01206                     'generation_after': generation_after,
01207                     'generation': generation_after,
01208                     'pending': read_pending('PENDING'),
01209                     'owners': [name for name, _ in owners],
01210                     'errors': list(errors)}
01211         attempts.append(snapshot)
01212         if release is not None:
01213             # CORRECTION 19 (M3): the release of the gates is **best
01214             # effort**: every gate is released on its own and a failure
01215             # of one gate never skips the gate that follows it.
01216             actions = (release if isinstance(release, (list, tuple))
01217                        else [release])
01218             for action in actions:
01219                 try:
01220                     action()
01221                 except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001
01222                     errors.append({'kind': 'RELEASE',
01223                                    'type': type(error).__name__,
01224                                    'detail': str(error)})
01225                     _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01226         remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
01227         salvage = _salvage_threads(owners, states=hints, seconds=seconds,
01228                                    total_seconds=remaining,
01229                                    interrupts=interrupts)
01230         # CORRECTION 18 (M3): **every** iteration of the drain is kept -
01231         # the failure of an earlier iteration is never erased by a later
01232         # iteration that ended the threads.
01233         all_salvage.extend(salvage)
01234         # The **final** exact-owner resnapshot: it is taken after the
01235         # joined producer threads of this iteration and it must agree
01236         # with the enumeration of this iteration.
01237         final_generation = read_generation('FINAL_GENERATION')
01238         try:
01239             final_owners, _final_hints = _registry_owners(
01240                 registry, session=session, channel=channel, prefix=prefix,
01241                 errors=errors, interrupts=interrupts)
01242         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it stays visible
01243             errors.append({'kind': 'FINAL_ENUMERATION',
01244                            'type': type(error).__name__,
01245                            'detail': str(error)})
01246             _note_interruption(interrupts, error)
01247             # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the final resnapshot never starts from
01248             # an empty list either: the **independently** known
01249             # references are the owners that the round really knows.
01250             final_owners = _observed_thread_refs(registry, errors, interrupts)
01251         # and the generation is read **again** after the final
01252         # resnapshot: a start that happened during it is a change of the
01253         # generation and the drain repeats.
01254         final_generation_after = read_generation('FINAL_GENERATION_AFTER')
01255         final_names = [name for name, _ in final_owners]
01256         missing = [name for name in snapshot['owners']
01257                    if name not in final_names]
01258         late = [name for name in final_names
01259                 if name not in snapshot['owners']]
01260         pending = read_pending('FINAL_PENDING')
01261         stable = (generation_before == generation_after
01262                   and final_generation == generation_after
01263                   and final_generation_after == generation_after
01264                   and not missing and not late)
01265         quiescent = (pending == 0 and stable
01266                      and _prove_salvage(salvage, prefix) is None)
01267         if quiescent or time.monotonic() >= deadline:
01268             break
01269         # the drain is **bounded** and it never spins: the next attempt
01270         # follows a short pause and it happens inside the same budget
01271         time.sleep(0.01)
01272     proof = _prove_salvage(all_salvage, prefix)
01273     if proof is None and errors:
01274         proof = '%s: the drain met %s' % (prefix, errors)
01275     # CORRECTION 19 (M2/M3): a round that is **not** quiescent is a
01276     # failure of the cleanup even when every owner that the salvage saw
01277     # was really joined: the final resnapshot of the drain found an
01278     # owner that no join released, and the proof of the cleanup says so.
01279     if proof is None and not quiescent:
01280         proof = ('%s: the owners of the round were not quiescent' % prefix)
01281     return {'owners': owners, 'hints': hints, 'salvage': salvage,
01282             'all_salvage': all_salvage,
01283             'attempts': attempts, 'proof': proof, 'errors': errors,
01284             'quiescent': bool(quiescent)}
01285 
01286 
01287 
01288 # -- N10-R01: the control handle of a process ------------------------------
01289 
01290 def test_n10_r01_the_control_handle_is_closed_only_after_the_exit(tmp_path):
01291     """N10-R01 (M03): the last control handle of a process is kept.
01292 
01293     Obligation: a Core that took a process and could not assign it to
01294     the job of the round - the child is still suspended and it never ran
01295     - keeps the **original** control handle of that process when the
01296     termination of the child fails and when the wait does not confirm
01297     the exit: the record of the round and the pending table of the
01298     round **both** forbid the close of the handle, the handle stays with
01299     its owner and the same owner may still terminate the process and
01300     wait for it inside the original deadline.  The thread handle and
01301     the pipe handles are independent of the exit and they are released
01302     on their own.
01303 
01304     Entry: ``BrokerCore._launch_role`` (the assignment of the process),
01305     ``BrokerCore._terminate_owned``, ``BrokerCore._release_the_records``,
01306     ``BrokerCore._process_closeable`` and
01307     ``_PendingResources.close_all``.  Trigger: the assignment of the
01308     process to the job fails, the termination of the child fails and the
01309     wait does not confirm the exit.  Oracle: the close of the control
01310     handle was never attempted, the handle is still reachable and the
01311     round is not a release.  Negative: a close that would succeed is
01312     never a proof that the process ended.  Returned state: the record
01313     and the pending table name the handle and its owner.  Salvage: the
01314     same owner confirms the exit later and closes the handle exactly
01315     once.
01316     """
01317     class UnconfirmedWin32(RecordingWin32):
01318         """A façade whose processes never confirm their exit by themselves."""
01319 
01320         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
01321             super().__init__(**kwargs)
01322             self.confirmed = False
01323 
01324         def terminate_job(self, job, exit_code):
01325             # The termination of the job does **not** mark the processes
01326             # as exited: only the wait of the round may confirm it.
01327             self.calls.append(('terminate_job', job, exit_code))
01328 
01329         def wait_process(self, process, timeout_ms):
01330             self.calls.append(('wait_process', process, timeout_ms))
01331             for entry in self.created:
01332                 if entry['handle'] == process:
01333                     entry['exited'] = self.confirmed
01334                     return entry['exited']
01335             return True
01336 
01337     win32 = UnconfirmedWin32(fail_at=('assign', 'terminate'))
01338     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01339     try:
01340         core.arm()
01341         # The assignment of the process to the job of the round fails:
01342         # the child is still suspended and it never ran.
01343         with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
01344             core.launch()
01345         assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_ASSIGN_FAILED'
01346         assert win32.fired == {'assign', 'terminate'}, \
01347             'the named failures of the round never happened'
01348         record = core.records[0]
01349         target = record['process_handle']
01350         assert target is not None, 'the child never got a control handle'
01351         facts = {'assigned': record['assigned'], 'resumed': record['resumed'],
01352                  'exited': record['exited'],
01353                  'process_handle_kept': record['process_handle'] is target,
01354                  'process_handle_closed': record['process_handle_closed'],
01355                  'thread_handle_closed': record['thread_handle_closed'],
01356                  'pending': [item['kind'] for item in core._pending.pending()],
01357                  'live': sorted(win32.live),
01358                  'state': core.state}
01359         write_phase('r01-first', facts)
01360         # The child never ran and its exit was never confirmed.
01361         assert record['assigned'] is False
01362         assert record['resumed'] is False
01363         assert record['exited'] is False
01364         # The control handle of the child is **still** with its owner.
01365         assert record['process_handle'] is target, \
01366             'the control handle was forgotten while the exit was unknown'
01367         assert record['process_handle_closed'] is False
01368         assert target in win32.live, \
01369             'the control handle was closed while the exit was unknown'
01370         # The pending table forbids the close as well: the eligibility
01371         # comes from the owner of the handle and not from the name of a
01372         # state.
01373         pending = [item for item in core._pending.pending()
01374                    if item['handle'] == target]
01375         assert pending, 'the control handle lost its owner'
01376         assert pending[0]['kind'] == 'process'
01377         # The thread handle is independent of the exit of the process.
01378         assert record['thread_handle_closed'] is True
01379         assert record['thread_handle'] is None
01380         # The round is not a release.
01381         assert core.state == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
01382         assert core._released is False
01383 
01384         # The same owner confirms the exit: the handle is closed once.
01385         win32.confirmed = True
01386         core.stop('STOP')
01387         facts = {'process_handle_closed': record['process_handle_closed'],
01388                  'closes': win32.closed.count(target),
01389                  'state': core.state,
01390                  'pending': [item['kind'] for item in core._pending.pending()]}
01391         write_phase('r01-second', facts)
01392         assert record['process_handle_closed'] is True
01393         assert record['process_handle'] is None
01394         assert win32.closed.count(target) == 1, \
01395             'the control handle was closed twice'
01396         assert target not in win32.live
01397     finally:
01398         # The salvage of the owner of the test is bounded and it never
01399         # hides a resource that the product kept.
01400         for handle in list(win32.live):
01401             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
01402 
01403 
01404 # -- N10-R02: the claim of the recovery and the handover -------------------
01405 
01406 def test_n10_r02_the_claim_of_the_recovery_and_the_handover_are_exclusive():
01407     """N10-R02 (M05): the recovery and the handover are one decision.
01408 
01409     Obligation: the recovery of the API owner **claims** the entry
01410     under the lock of the table before it touches it and the handover
01411     of the same entry is **refused** while the claim is alive: a
01412     handle that was handed over may never be closed by the recovery and
01413     a handle that the recovery claimed may never be handed over.  The
01414     blocking primitive of the recovery runs **outside** of the lock of
01415     the table and the table stays usable while it waits.  An exception
01416     never leaves an entry without an owner and never leaves two close
01417     owners.
01418 
01419     Entry: ``WindowsApi.release_retained``, ``WindowsApi.handover`` and
01420     ``WindowsApi._claim_entry``.  Trigger: the recovery blocks inside
01421     the close of one end of a pipe while the core hands the other end
01422     over.  Oracle: the handover of the claimed end is refused and the
01423     end is never registered with a second owner; the handover of the
01424     other end succeeds while the recovery waits.  Negative: the table
01425     lock is never held while the recovery waits.  Returned state: the
01426     report of the recovery names the entries that it did not own.
01427     Salvage: the recovery of the API owner releases the ends that it
01428     owns.
01429 
01430     CORRECTION 11 (M05): the **creation** of a process and the
01431     recovery of the façade are **one** ownership decision as well.  The
01432     original entries of the process and of its thread are reserved or
01433     refused under the lock of the table, the blocking primitives run
01434     outside of that lock, and a handle that the recovery of the façade
01435     claimed is never delivered to the core and never closed twice.
01436     The group drives the race between the birth query of a created
01437     process and the recovery of the façade and it proves that there is
01438     exactly one closer of the original handle of the process.
01439     """
01440     entered = threading.Event()
01441     release = threading.Event()
01442     closed = []
01443 
01444     def create_pipe(kernel32, *args):
01445         read = kernel32.allocate('pipe_read')
01446         write = kernel32.allocate('pipe_write')
01447         args[0]._obj.value = read
01448         args[1]._obj.value = write
01449         return 1
01450 
01451     def close_handle(kernel32, *args):
01452         handle = getattr(args[0], 'value', args[0])
01453         if handle in kernel32.live and kernel32.live[handle] == 'pipe_read':
01454             # The close of the read end blocks: the recovery holds its
01455             # claim while it waits.
01456             entered.set()
01457             release.wait(10.0)
01458         kernel32.live.pop(handle, None)
01459         closed.append(handle)
01460         return 1
01461 
01462     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe,
01463                                          'CloseHandle': close_handle})
01464     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
01465     read, write = api.create_pipe('stdout')
01466     report_holder = {}
01467 
01468     def recover():
01469         report_holder['report'] = api.release_retained()
01470 
01471     worker = threading.Thread(target=recover, name='lvw-n10-r02-recovery')
01472     worker.start()
01473     # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the release of the gate of the recovery is
01474     # guarded: a cancellation of it never skips the join that follows it
01475     # and it never replaces the failure that is already unwinding.
01476     recovery_cleanup_errors = []
01477     recovery_interrupts = []
01478     try:
01479         assert entered.wait(10.0), \
01480             'the recovery never reached the close of the claimed end'
01481         # The table is usable while the recovery waits: the lock of the
01482         # table is not held by the blocking primitive.
01483         registered = []
01484 
01485         def register(items):
01486             registered.extend(items)
01487 
01488         # The handover of the **claimed** end is refused.
01489         with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
01490             api.handover([(read, 'pipe_read')], owner='CORE', register=register)
01491         assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_HANDOVER_CLAIMED'
01492         assert registered == [], \
01493             'a claimed end was registered with a second owner'
01494         # The handover of the **other** end succeeds: the table is not
01495         # held by the recovery that waits.
01496         api.handover([(write, 'pipe_write')], owner='CORE', register=register)
01497         assert registered == [(write, 'pipe_write')]
01498         write_phase('r02-claim', {'claimed': repr(read),
01499                                    'refused_code': info.value.code,
01500                                    'registered': [repr(item)
01501                                                   for item in registered]})
01502     finally:
01503         # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the release of the gate is guarded and the
01504         # exact object of a cancellation of it is retained.
01505         _release_gate(release.set, recovery_cleanup_errors, recovery_interrupts)
01506         worker.join(15.0)
01507         # the **exact** cancellation wins over the ordinary assertion of
01508         # the join that follows it.
01509         _raise_first_interruption(recovery_interrupts)
01510         assert not worker.is_alive(), 'the recovery was left behind'
01511     report = report_holder.get('report') or []
01512     write_phase('r02-report', {'report': [{'kind': item['kind'],
01513                                             'released': item['released']}
01514                                            for item in report],
01515                                'closed': list(closed)})
01516     # The claimed end was closed by the recovery that owned it and the
01517     # end that was handed over was never closed by the recovery.
01518     assert closed.count(getattr(read, 'value', read)) == 1
01519     assert closed.count(getattr(write, 'value', write)) == 0, \
01520         'the recovery closed an end that it handed over'
01521 
01522     # The second direction: an end that was handed over is never closed
01523     # by a recovery that read the entry before the handover.
01524     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe})
01525     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
01526     read, write = api.create_pipe('stderr')
01527     key = id(read)
01528     entry = api._retained.get(key)
01529     assert entry is not None, 'the end was not retained by the facade'
01530     registered = []
01531     api.handover([(read, 'pipe_read'), (write, 'pipe_write')], owner='CORE',
01532                  register=lambda items: registered.extend(items))
01533     assert registered == [(read, 'pipe_read'), (write, 'pipe_write')]
01534     # The recovery of the facade finds nothing: the end has an owner.
01535     report = api.release_retained()
01536     write_phase('r02-handed-over', {'report': report,
01537                                      'live': sorted(kernel32.live)})
01538     assert report == [], 'the recovery closed an end that it handed over'
01539     assert getattr(read, 'value', read) in kernel32.live
01540 
01541     # -- the third direction (N1 correction 11, M05): the **creation**
01542     #    of a process and the recovery of the façade are one ownership
01543     #    decision.  The birth query of the created process blocks, the
01544     #    recovery of the façade claims the original entries of the
01545     #    process and of its thread while the creation is in flight,
01546     #    and the return of the creation may then neither deliver a
01547     #    stale claimed handle to the core nor forget it.  There is
01548     #    exactly **one** closer of the original handle of the process.
01549     blocked = threading.Event()
01550     resume = threading.Event()
01551     closed_handles = []
01552 
01553     def get_process_times(kernel32, *args):
01554         # The birth query of the created process blocks: the recovery
01555         # of the façade runs while the creation is in flight.
01556         blocked.set()
01557         resume.wait(10.0)
01558         # The production hands the birth query a **reference** to the
01559         # structure of the creation.  The structure itself is the
01560         # ``_obj`` of that reference: a field that is written on the
01561         # reference itself would raise before the race of the group was
01562         # ever reached.
01563         creation = structure_of(args[1])
01564         creation.dwLowDateTime = kernel32.creation_time & 0xFFFFFFFF
01565         creation.dwHighDateTime = kernel32.creation_time >> 32
01566         return 1
01567 
01568     def close_handle(kernel32, *args):
01569         handle = getattr(args[0], 'value', args[0])
01570         closed_handles.append(handle)
01571         kernel32.live.pop(handle, None)
01572         return 1
01573 
01574     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'GetProcessTimes': get_process_times,
01575                                          'CloseHandle': close_handle})
01576     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
01577     outcome = {}
01578 
01579     def create():
01580         try:
01581             outcome['created'] = api.create_process_suspended(
01582                 executable='C:/python.exe', arguments=['-I'],
01583                 working_directory='C:/', stdout_handle=0, stderr_handle=0)
01584         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01585             outcome['error'] = error
01586 
01587     creator = threading.Thread(target=create, name='lvw-n10-r02-creation')
01588     creator.start()
01589     # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the release of the gate of the creation is
01590     # guarded: a cancellation of it never skips the join that follows it
01591     # and it never replaces the failure that is already unwinding.
01592     creation_cleanup_errors = []
01593     creation_interrupts = []
01594     try:
01595         assert blocked.wait(10.0), \
01596             'the birth query of the creation never ran'
01597         # The recovery of the façade claims the original entries of the
01598         # process and of its thread while the creation is in flight.
01599         report = api.release_retained()
01600         assert report, 'the recovery of the façade found nothing to release'
01601         resume.set()
01602         creator.join(15.0)
01603         assert not creator.is_alive(), 'the creation was left behind'
01604     finally:
01605         # CORRECTION 20 (M3): the release of the gate is guarded and the
01606         # exact object of a cancellation of it is retained.
01607         _release_gate(resume.set, creation_cleanup_errors, creation_interrupts)
01608         creator.join(15.0)
01609     # the **exact** cancellation of the cleanup wins over an ordinary
01610     # assertion and it is re-raised as **itself**.
01611     _raise_first_interruption(creation_interrupts)
01612     facts = {'error': getattr(outcome.get('error'), 'code', None),
01613              'created': outcome.get('created') is not None,
01614              'closed': list(closed_handles),
01615              'report': [{'kind': item['kind'], 'released': item['released']}
01616                         for item in report]}
01617     write_phase('r02-creation', facts)
01618     # A stale claimed handle is never delivered to the core.
01619     assert outcome.get('created') is None, \
01620         'a stale claimed handle was delivered to the core'
01621     error = outcome.get('error')
01622     assert isinstance(error, BrokerError), \
01623         'the creation did not refuse the refused handover'
01624     assert error.code == 'BROKER_PROCESS_HANDOVER_REFUSED'
01625     # There is exactly **one** closer of the original handle of the
01626     # process and of its thread: the recovery of the façade and the
01627     # direct cleanup of the failed creation are one owner.
01628     assert closed_handles.count(kernel32.process_handle) == 1, \
01629         'the original handle of the process was closed twice'
01630     assert closed_handles.count(kernel32.thread_handle) == 1, \
01631         'the original handle of the thread was closed twice'
01632 
01633 
01634 # -- N10-R03: one notification for every confirmed close -------------------
01635 
01636 def test_n10_r03_every_confirmed_close_notifies_the_owner_of_the_handle(tmp_path):
01637     """N10-R03 (M06): the notification of a close is one notification.
01638 
01639     Obligation: a reader that really stopped and whose read end could
01640     not be closed by the first attempt is closed by the pending pass of
01641     the round, and that confirmed close notifies the **owner** of the
01642     handle: the reader learns that its handle is gone, the pending
01643     table and the retained resources of the round agree with it, and
01644     the next stop of the round never closes the same original handle a
01645     second time - a handle that the operating system may have reused.
01646 
01647     Entry: ``BrokerCore._close_one_reader``,
01648     ``BrokerCore._note_closed_handle`` and
01649     ``_PendingResources.close_all``.  Trigger: the first close of the
01650     read end of a stopped reader fails and the pending pass succeeds.
01651     Oracle: the reader holds no handle any more, the pending table does
01652     not name it and the handle was closed exactly once.  Negative: a
01653     second stop never closes the handle again.  Returned state: the
01654     facts of the two stops agree with each other.  Salvage: the owner of
01655     the test releases the handles that the product kept.
01656     """
01657     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01658     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01659     try:
01660         core.arm()
01661         core.launch()
01662         reader = core.readers[0]
01663         target = reader.handle
01664         assert target is not None, 'the reader never got a handle'
01665         state = {'failed': False}
01666         original_close = win32.close_handle
01667 
01668         def close_handle(handle):
01669             if handle == target and not state['failed']:
01670                 # The first close of the read end of the reader fails.
01671                 state['failed'] = True
01672                 win32.calls.append(('close_handle', handle))
01673                 raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
01674             original_close(handle)
01675 
01676         win32.close_handle = close_handle
01677         core.stop('STOP')
01678         first = {'reader_handle': reader.handle,
01679                  'closed': win32.closed.count(target),
01680                  'pending': [item['handle'] for item in core._pending.pending()],
01681                  'retained': [item['handle']
01682                               for item in core._retained_resources],
01683                  'state': core.state}
01684         write_phase('r03-first', first)
01685         assert state['failed'], 'the targeted close never failed'
01686         # The confirmed close of the pending pass notified the owner.
01687         assert reader.handle is None, \
01688             'the reader still names a handle that was really closed'
01689         assert target not in [item['handle'] for item in core._pending.pending()]
01690         assert target not in [item['handle']
01691                               for item in core._retained_resources]
01692         assert win32.closed.count(target) == 1
01693 
01694         # The next stop of the round never closes the same handle again.
01695         core.stop('STOP')
01696         second = {'reader_handle': reader.handle,
01697                   'closed': win32.closed.count(target),
01698                   'state': core.state}
